I now have a complete picture. Here is the structured verdict:

---

## ISSUES FOUND

**Phase:** 35-phala-testnet-tee-migration
**Plans checked:** 5
**Issues:** 3 blocker(s), 4 warning(s)

---

### Success Criterion Coverage

| SC | Description | Coverage | Plans |
|----|-------------|----------|-------|
| SC-1 | TEE worker deployed as Phala CVM with `TEE_MODE=cvm`, hardware key derivation | COVERED | 35-02 (code), 35-03 (CI/CD), 35-05 (deploy) |
| SC-2 | Staging API completes IPNS republish cycles end-to-end | PARTIAL — Task 3 in 35-05 is a `checkpoint:human-verify` with no concrete republish verification steps | 35-03, 35-05 |
| SC-3 | Epoch persistence across worker restarts | PARTIAL — deterministic derivation means persistence is implicit; no explicit restart-and-verify step exists | 35-02, 35-05 |
| SC-4 | Republish latency captured as new staging baselines (< 2x simulator) | GAP — zero tasks address latency measurement or baseline capture | None |
| SC-5 | Staging docker-compose no longer runs local `tee-worker` | COVERED | 35-03 Task 1 |
| CONCERNS.md test gap | Unit tests for key derivation, ECIES, IPNS signing, auth, republish | COVERED | 35-01 |
| STACK.md update | Document Phala CVM, dstack SDK | COVERED | 35-04 Task 1 |
| ENVIRONMENTS.md update | Document staging TEE migration | COVERED | 35-04 Task 2 |

---

### Blockers (must fix)

**1. [requirement_coverage] SC-4 (latency baselines) has no covering task in any plan**

The success criterion explicitly states: "Republish latency on Phala testnet is within acceptable bounds (< 2x simulator latency per batch) and captured as new staging baselines." No plan contains a task that (a) measures republish latency against the new CVM endpoint, (b) compares it to prior baselines, or (c) captures a new baseline. VALIDATION.md acknowledges this as "Manual Grafana histogram comparison" but defers it to a TBD task with no plan assignment.

- Plan: None
- Fix: Add a task to 35-05 (or a new 35-06 plan) that: reads Phase 34 baseline numbers from ROADMAP/STATE, runs a staging republish against the live CVM, checks Grafana for the histogram, records the new baseline, and declares pass/fail against the < 2x threshold. If this is purely manual, document the exact Grafana query and expected numbers in the task action so the human-verify checkpoint has concrete criteria.

---

**2. [nyquist_compliance] VALIDATION.md has `nyquist_compliant: false` and `wave_0_complete: false` — Wave 0 test files are listed as requirements but plans assign them to Wave 1**

VALIDATION.md explicitly marks itself non-compliant. Its Wave 0 requirements list four test files (`tee-keys.test.ts`, `key-manager.test.ts`, `ipns-signer.test.ts`, `auth.test.ts`) that must exist before dependent tasks run. Plan 35-01 creates those files but is classified as Wave 1 — the same wave as Plan 35-02 which modifies `tee-keys.ts` (the subject under test). This means 35-02 could execute before 35-01, running `npx tsc --noEmit` without any test infrastructure, and subsequently the tests in 35-01 would test a version of `tee-keys.ts` whose CVM code path was already changed by 35-02's parallel execution. The intended Wave 0 / Wave 1 separation is broken.

- Plans: 35-01, 35-02
- Fix: Either (a) assign 35-01 to Wave 0 and add `depends_on: ["35-01"]` to 35-02, or (b) update VALIDATION.md to reflect that `nyquist_compliant: true` is satisfied by 35-01 being the first plan executed in Wave 1 before 35-02 can start — but this requires the parallelization config to be set so 35-01 and 35-02 run sequentially in Wave 1. Given `parallelization: true` in config.json, plans in the same wave WILL run concurrently. Option (a) is the correct fix.

---

**3. [key_links_planned / requirement_coverage] SC-2 end-to-end republish cycle verification has no concrete steps**

Plan 35-05 Task 3 is `checkpoint:human-verify` with `<action>` containing only "The user needs to verify the setup is correct." — no concrete verification steps for an actual IPNS republish cycle (enroll → POST /republish → verify IPNS record resolves). The `<done>` criteria says "User confirmed: CVM health returns mode=cvm" but CVM health alone does not confirm that republish cycles (enroll, sign, publish) complete end-to-end.

- Plan: 35-05, Task 3
- Fix: Expand Task 3's `<action>` with concrete end-to-end steps: trigger a staging republish job (via API or direct HTTP), verify the batch returns `success: true` for at least one entry, optionally verify the IPNS name resolves to the signed CID. Alternatively add these as named sub-checks in the `<done>` criteria so the human-verify checkpoint is not a rubber stamp.

---

### Warnings (should fix)

**4. [dependency_correctness] Plan 35-04 (docs) runs in Wave 1 but documents state created by Wave 1 code changes (35-02)**

STACK.md Task 1 in 35-04 reads `tee-worker/package.json` to document "actual dependencies after 35-02 installs dstack-sdk." If 35-04 and 35-02 run concurrently in Wave 1, the docs agent may read package.json before 35-02 installs `@phala/dstack-sdk`, producing docs that reference a version that wasn't yet installed when the docs were written.

- Plan: 35-04
- Fix: Add `depends_on: ["35-02"]` to 35-04 and move it to Wave 2. This ensures docs are written after the code reflects the actual installed version.

---

**5. [scope_sanity / key_links_planned] Plan 35-01 and 35-02 both modify `tee-worker/package.json` and `tee-worker/package-lock.json` in the same wave**

Both plans list `tee-worker/package.json` and `tee-worker/package-lock.json` in `files_modified`. With `parallelization: true`, both agents will `npm install` into the same directory concurrently — 35-01 installs `vitest @vitest/coverage-v8 supertest @types/supertest` while 35-02 installs `@phala/dstack-sdk`. Concurrent `npm install` runs in the same directory produce lock file conflicts.

- Plans: 35-01, 35-02
- Fix: Add `depends_on: ["35-01"]` to 35-02 (moving it to Wave 2), or consolidate all npm installs into a single plan and have the other depend on it. Either way, sequential install ordering eliminates the lock file conflict.

---

**6. [sc-3 coverage] SC-3 (epoch state persists across worker restarts) is verified only implicitly**

SC-3's "persists across worker restarts" claim is technically satisfied by CVM deterministic key derivation (same `app_id` + epoch path → same key, no stored state to lose). However, the plans contain no explicit restart-and-verify step. If the CVM has a bug (e.g., epoch counter stored in-memory and reset on restart), this would not be caught. Plan 35-05 Task 2 checks health and public key on first boot only.

- Plan: 35-05
- Fix: Add to Task 2 or Task 3: after initial CVM deployment, call `/public-key?epoch=1`, restart the container or redeploy, call `/public-key?epoch=1` again, and assert the public key bytes are identical. This confirms deterministic derivation survives restarts and provides an explicit SC-3 test.

---

**7. [task_completeness] VALIDATION.md `wave_0_complete: false` and `nyquist_compliant: false` are self-reported flags that are never flipped**

The VALIDATION.md sign-off checklist includes `[ ] nyquist_compliant: true set in frontmatter` but there is no task in any plan that updates these flags after Wave 0 completes. If the executor forgets to update VALIDATION.md, the file permanently reports the phase as non-compliant.

- Plan: None (cross-cutting)
- Fix: Add acceptance criteria to 35-01's Task 2 `<done>` block: "Update VALIDATION.md: set `wave_0_complete: true`" — or add it as a step in the plan's `<verification>` block.

---

### Structured Issues

---

### Recommendation

3 blockers require revision before execution can begin.

**Priority order:**
1. Fix the Wave 0 / Wave 1 conflict between 35-01 and 35-02 (concurrent npm install + Nyquist compliance). Assign 35-01 to Wave 0.
2. Add an SC-4 latency baseline task to 35-05 or create a 35-06 plan — even if it is a human-verify checkpoint, it must have concrete Grafana query steps and pass/fail criteria.
3. Strengthen plan 35-05 Task 3's end-to-end verification action with concrete republish cycle steps so SC-2 is genuinely verified, not just rubber-stamped.

The warnings about plan 35-04's Wave 1 timing and the package.json concurrent-install conflict are entangled with the blocker fix — resolving the Wave 0 assignment for 35-01 naturally forces 35-02 into Wave 2, which also eliminates the lockfile conflict and creates a natural ordering for 35-04 to read the installed package.json.

Measure CVM latency with the same payload:\n   ```bash\n   # Run 5 times to get average\n   for i in 1 2 3 4 5; do\n     time curl -s -X POST \"https://{cvm-endpoint}/republish\" \\\n       -H \"Authorization: Bearer ${TEE_WORKER_SECRET}\" \\\n       -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n       -d '{\"entries\": [{\"encryptedIpnsKey\": \"...\", \"keyEpoch\": 1, \"currentEpoch\": 1, \"previousEpoch\": null, \"ipnsName\": \"k51...\", \"latestCid\": \"bafybeigdyrzt5sfp7udm7hu76uh7y26nf3efuylqabf3oclgtqy55fbzdi\", \"sequenceNumber\": \"'$i'\"}]}'\n     echo \"\"\n   done\n   ```\n\n3. Compare: CVM average latency should be < 2x simulator average latency.\n   - Simulator baseline (local Docker): expected ~50-200ms per batch\n   - CVM (network hop + TEE overhead): expected ~200-800ms per batch\n   - If CVM latency > 2x simulator: flag as concern but not necessarily blocking (network latency to Phala Cloud is inherent)\n\n4. Record the new baseline in `.planning/STATE.md` or the phase summary:\n   ```\n   TEE Republish Latency Baselines (Phase 35):\n   - Simulator (local Docker): {N}ms avg per batch\n   - Phala CVM (testnet): {N}ms avg per batch\n   - Ratio: {N}x\n   - Verdict: PASS / ACCEPTABLE / NEEDS INVESTIGATION\n   ```\n\n5. If Grafana is available, check the `republish_duration_seconds` histogram for the staging environment.\n  </action>\n  <verify>\n    <automated>echo \"Human-verify: user confirms latency baselines captured and < 2x threshold met\"</automated>\n  </verify>\n  <acceptance_criteria>\n    - Latency measurements recorded for at least 3 CVM republish calls\n    - Baseline comparison documented (simulator vs CVM)\n    - Verdict recorded as PASS (< 2x) or documented reason if exceeding threshold\n  </acceptance_criteria>\n  <done>User confirmed: republish latency baselines captured, CVM latency within acceptable bounds -- SC-4 satisfied</done>\n</task>"
}
